Vol: 48 16th - 22nd October 2026 ISSN 1391 - 049 No.42
RETAIL MARKET AT A GLANCE
Summary
Rice
The retail prices of local samba and local keeri sama have increased
while the prices of local nadu, raw red, raw white and imported samba
have decreased. Compared to the same period of the last year, the highest
price decrease of 13% was recorded for local keeri samba.
(Page No.01)
Other Field Crops
The retail prices of both local and imported onions have increased.
Further, the retail prices of cowpea and red dhal have increased while the
retail prices of imported dry chilli and green gram have decreased.
However, compared to the same period of the last year, the highest price
increases of 146% was recorded for imported dry chilli.
(Page No.02-03)
Vegetables
The retail prices of most of the vegetable varieties have increased.
Considering the up country vegetables, the highest price increase o f
Rs.110.00/kg was reported for tomato while considering the low country
vegetables, the highest price increase of Rs.106.00/kg was recorded for
capsicum. Further, compared to the same period of thelast year, retail
prices of most of the vegetable varieties have increased and the highest
price increase of 258% was recorded for tomato.
(Page No.03-05)
Fruits
The highest price decrease of Rs.166.00/kg was reported fo r
karthakolomban mango while the highest price increase of Rs.113.00/kg
was reported for slime apple. However, compared to the same period of
the last year, the highest price increase of 62% was reported for koliluttu
banana.
(Page No.05-06)
Fish
The retail prices of most of the fish varieties have increased compared to
the previous week. The highest price increase of Rs.510.00/kg was
recorded for balaya. However, compared to the same period of the last
year, the highest price increase of 34% was recorded for salaya.
(Page No.06)

Weekly Food Commodities Bulletin

NOTE
The Food Commodities Bulletin is a weekly
publication containing information relating
to wholesale and retail prices of selected
food commodities in nine selected markets
in and around Colombo and main markets
in the major producing areas.
The information is analyzed and presented
as prevailing prices, price ranges, averages,
and a comparison of weekly prices. The
changes in prices reported are always with
price prevailed during the previous week
unless otherwise stated.
The frequency of collection of wholesale
price information is twice a week (i.e.,
Tuesday and Friday) while with retail
prices, it is thrice a week (i.e., Friday,
Monday, and Tuesday).
8

Vol: 47 14th - 20th November 2025 ISSN 1391 - 049 No.46
RETAIL MARKET AT A GLANCE
Summary
Paddy/Rice
Retail prices of all the local rice varieties have increased
with the highest price increase of Rs.5.00/kg was noted
for local keeri samba.
The average farmgate prices of major paddy varieties
except long grain red paddy have not changed
significantly. Prices of long grain red paddy have
increased by Rs.8.00/kg in Matara and Rs.2.00/kg in
Embilipitiya.
(Page No.01-02)
Other Field Crops
Price of vedalan red onion has decreased while the price
of imported red onion has increased. Prices of both local
and imported big onion have increased.
Retail prices of both Welimada and Nuwara Eliya potato
have increased while the price of imported potato has
decreased.
(Page No.02-04)
Vegetables
Price of most of the upcountry and low country
vegetables have decreased due high supply from main
producing areas. Among the up-country vegetables, the
highest price decrease was noted for carrots by
Rs.45.00/kg.
(Page No.04-05)
Coconut
Prices of both large and small nuts have increased by
around Rs.2.00/nut. Price ranged Rs.180.00-215.00 for
large nuts and Rs.175.00-190.00 for a small nut.
(Page No.05)

Weekly Food Commodities Bulletin
Meat
Retail
Prices of all meat types, except for pork and
beef have increased. The highest price
increase was recorded for mutton by
Rs.14.00/kg. highest price decrease
was recorded for pork by Rs.25.00/kg
respectively.
Compared to the same period of last year,
prices of all meat varieties have increased.
The highest price increase was recorded for
pork by 117%.
NOTE
The Food Commodities Bulletin is a weekly
publication containing information relating
to wholesale and retail prices of selected
food commodities in nine selected markets
in and around Colombo and main markets
in the major producing areas.
The information is analyzed and presented
as prevailing prices, price ranges, averages,
and a comparison of weekly prices. The
changes in prices reported are always with
price prevailed during the previous week
unless otherwise stated.
The frequency of collection of wholesale
price information is twice a week (i.e.,
Tuesday and Friday) while with retail
prices, it is thrice a week (i.e., Friday,
Monday, and Tuesday).
